\displaystyle=\frac{(\widetilde{A}_{m}-\widetilde{A}_{m}(0)-\nabla_{v}\widetilde{A}_{m}(0)\cdot(r_{m}v))\widetilde{p}_{0,m,r_{m}}}{r_{m}^{2+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},\,F_{m,0,2,2}=\frac{\nabla_{v}\widetilde{A}_{m}(0)\cdot(r_{m}v)\widetilde{p}_{0,m,r_{m}}-3\widetilde{q}_{1,m,r_{m}}}{r_{m}^{2+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},

\displaystyle F_{m,1,2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\widetilde{A}_{m}-\widetilde{A}_{m}(0))\widetilde{p}_{1,m,r_{m}}}{r_{m}^{2+\epsilon}\theta(r_{m})},

where
m_{0}
is given in (
5.28
),
m_{1}\coloneqq 0
and the constants
\alpha_{i}
are determined in (
5.6
).
Moreover, by (
5.27
), we have

(5.33)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{F}_{m}]_{C^{1+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{R})}\leq c\frac{(1+|a_{m,r_{m}}|)}{\theta(r_{m})}

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,R)
. Now employing
subsection 5.1
together with the first inequality in (
5.29
), (
5.31
) and (
5.33
) yields the second inequality in (
5.29
). Moreover, as in the proof of
C^{1,\epsilon}
estimates given in
[
38
, Lemma 2.5]
, we have uniform
C^{1,\epsilon}
estimates in (
5.29
).

•

k=0
. In this case, the coefficient
A
is not differentiable, so we consider divergence type data on the right-hand side. Since the computations are similar to the case
k=1
, we will not give details. By using (
2.7
) and the fact that
\widetilde{p}_{0,m,r_{m}}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\widetilde{p}_{1,m,r_{m}}=\widetilde{p}_{3,m,r_{m}}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}_{m}(z)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=0}^{1}\left[\mathrm{div}_{v}(G_{m,i,0}{\varphi_{2i}})+\mathrm{div}_{v}(G_{m,i,1}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{2i}}))+F_{m,i,0}{\varphi_{2i}}+F_{m,i,1}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{2i}}\right]+r_{m}^{2}\widetilde{p}_{2,m,r_{m}},

where

(5.34)

G_{m,0,0}(0)=G_{m,0,1}(0)=G_{m,1,1}(0)=0,\quad G_{m,1,0}=F_{m,1,0}=0

and for any
R\leq 1/r_{m}

(5.35)

\displaystyle\|G_{m,0,0}\|_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{R})}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}(\|G_{m,i,1}\|_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{R})}+\|F_{m,i,0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R})})+\|F_{m,0,1}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R})}\leq c\frac{\|p_{0,m,r_{m}}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}}{\theta(r_{m})}

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,R)
. Recalling (
5.27
), we have

(5.36)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{F}_{m}]_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{R})}\leq c\frac{(1+|a_{m,r_{m}}|)}{\theta(r_{m})}

for some constant
c=c(n,R,\Lambda,\epsilon)
. With these estimates at hand, together with the first inequality given in (
5.29
), we apply
subsection 5.1
to see that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{F}_{m}+r_{m}^{2}\widetilde{p}_{2,m,r_{m}}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R})}\leq c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,R).

Thus, using
subsection 5.1
, the second inequality given in (
5.29
) follows. Furthermore, as in the case
k=1
, we obtain the third inequality in (
5.29
).

Lastly, using the standard energy estimate and
subsection 5.1
together with (
5.31
), (
5.33
), (
5.35
) and (
5.36
), we deduce

(5.37)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{R/2}}|\nabla_{v}f|^{2}\,dz\leq c(n,\Lambda,R,\epsilon,k),

which is used for the convergence of the drift term in the next step.

Step 3. A limit of the blow up sequence.

Now we are ready to apply [Lemma D.1, KW 26] together with (
5.29
) -(
5.37
) to see that
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\widetilde{f}_{m}
solves

(5.38)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\widetilde{f}_{\infty}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{f}_{\infty}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}_{\infty}\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{\infty})&=m_{1}+\sum_{i=0}^{2}p_{i}\partial^{(i)}_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}>0\}$},\\
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\\
\|\widetilde{f}_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{R})}&\leq cR^{\frac{3}{2}+k+\epsilon}&&\qquad\mbox{for any $R\geq 1$},\end{aligned}\right.

for some constants
m_{1},c\in\mathbb{R}
and some polynomials
p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{i}
with
p_{1}(0)=p_{2}(0)=Dp_{2}(0)=0
. In particular, when
k+\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
, then it holds
m_{1}=0
, and when
k+\epsilon<1
, then we have
p_{0}=p_{1}=p_{2}=0
.
To prove (
5.38
), we have also used that
\widetilde{A}_{\infty}=\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}\widetilde{A}_{m}(0)
, as
\|A_{m}\|_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{1})}\leq\Lambda
. For the convergence of the source terms, when
k=1
, we used that by (
5.31
) and (
5.33
), it holds

(5.39)

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}_{m}+\widetilde{F}_{m}\to\sum_{i=0}^{1}F_{\infty,i,0}{\varphi_{i}}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}F_{\infty,i,1,2}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{i}}+F_{\infty,0,2,2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}+c_{\infty}

for some constants
F_{\infty,i,0},c_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
, some polynomials
F_{\infty,i,1,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
F_{\infty,0,2,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
for each
i\in\{0,1\}
. Moreover, using (
5.32
), we derive

\displaystyle F_{\infty,0,1,2}(0)=F_{\infty,1,1,2}(0)=F_{\infty,0,2,2}(0)=DF_{\infty,0,2,2}(0)=D^{2}F_{\infty,0,2,2}(0)=0.

Thus, recalling (
2.9
) and using that
\partial_{v_{n}}\varphi_{1}=\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}+v_{n}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}
, we can rewrite (
5.39
) as the right-hand side given in (
5.38
) by choosing
m_{1}
and
p_{0},p_{1},p_{2}
appropriately. Similarly, the case
k=0
can be derived in a similar way using (
5.35
), (
5.36
), and (
5.34
).

Now, from (
2.4
) and
subsection 5.1
, we deduce that there are polynomials
q_{0},q_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
with
q_{i}(0)=Dq_{i}(0)=0
and constants
q_{2},q_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\sum\limits_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)-\mathrm{div}_{v}\left(\widetilde{A}_{\infty}\nabla_{v}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)\right)=m_{0}+\sum_{i=0}^{2}p_{i}\partial^{(i)}_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}.

Note that
q_{2}=0
when
k+\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
,
q_{0}=q_{1}=q_{4}=0
when
k+\epsilon<1
.

Thus, altogether, we obtain that
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}
solves (
5.38
) with zero right-hand side. Hence, by the Liouville theorem given in
[
38
, Theorem 4.1]
applied with
p=q=0
, we derive

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\infty}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}=p{\varphi_{0}}+p_{0}{\varphi_{3}}+\bar{p}_{1}+\bar{p}_{2}\varphi_{2}

for some
p\in\mathcal{P}_{[1+k+\epsilon]}
,
p_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\bar{p}_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{[\frac{3}{2}+k+\epsilon]}
, and
\bar{p}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. However, since
\bar{p}_{1}
violates the boundary condition and
\bar{p}_{2}\varphi_{2}
violates the homogeneity of the source term, we conclude that

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\infty}-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{4}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}=p{\varphi_{0}}+p_{0}{\varphi_{3}}.

Now using
subsection 5.1
, we have
(\widetilde{A}_{\infty})_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}p(0)=0
. In addition, by
\widetilde{A}_{m}(0)\to\widetilde{A}_{\infty}
, there is a sequences of vectors
(\mathbf{Q}_{m})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\mathbf{Q}_{m}\to\nabla_{v}p(0)\quad\text{and}\quad(\widetilde{A}_{m}(0))_{n}\cdot\mathbf{Q}_{m}=0
. Thus, we have proved that

(5.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{\infty}=a_{0}{\varphi_{0}}+\sum_{i=0}^{3}q_{\infty,i}{\varphi_{i}},

where
a_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
q_{\infty,0},q_{\infty,1}\in\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
,
q_{\infty,2}\in\mathcal{P}_{[k+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}]}
,
q_{\infty,3}\in\mathcal{P}_{[k+\epsilon-1]}
with

(5.41)

\displaystyle q_{\infty,0}(0)=q_{\infty,1}(0)=Dq_{\infty,1}(0),

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{m}\to\nabla_{v}q_{\infty,0}(0)\quad\text{and}\quad(\widetilde{A}_{m}(0))_{n}\cdot\mathbf{Q}_{m}=0.

Recall that by (
5.24
) and the last condition in (
5.20
), the limit
c_{2,\infty}\coloneqq\lim\limits_{m\to\infty}c_{2,m}
exists for a suitable subsequence. Note that for any
z\in H_{1}
, we have

(5.42)

\displaystyle|r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2,m}(S_{r_{m}}z)-c_{2,\infty}{\varphi_{0}}(z)|

\displaystyle\leq|r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2,m}(S_{r_{m}}z)-c_{2,m}{\varphi_{0}}(z)|+|c_{2,m}-c_{2,\infty}|{\varphi_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle\leq r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{2,m}-c_{2,m}{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{r_{m}|z|})}+|c_{2,m}-c_{2,\infty}|{\varphi_{0}}(z),

where we have used the fact that
{\varphi_{0}}(S_{r}z)=r^{\frac{1}{2}}{\varphi_{0}}(z)
. Therefore using (
5.24
), (
5.42
) and the fact that
c_{2,m}\to c_{2,\infty}
, we have

(5.43)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\|{f_{2,m}(S_{r_{m}}z)}r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-c_{2,\infty}{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}=0.

In particular by the last condition given in (
5.20
), we have
c_{2,\infty}>0
.

Therefore, by (
5.22
), (
5.40
), (
5.41
), and (
5.43
), we have

\displaystyle 0=\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_{H_{1}}\widetilde{f}_{m}\left(\frac{a_{0}}{c_{2,\infty}}r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2}(S_{r_{m}}z)+\sum_{i=0}^{3}q_{m,i}{\varphi_{i}}(S_{r_{m}}z)\right)\,dz=\int_{H_{1}}(\widetilde{f}_{\infty})^{2},

where we write

\displaystyle q_{m,0}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{Q}_{m}\cdot v+q_{\infty}(z)-(\nabla_{v}q_{\infty,0}(0)\cdot v)),

\displaystyle q_{m,i}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq r_{m}^{-\alpha_{i}}q_{\infty,i}(z)\quad\text{for each }i\in\{1,2,3\}

to see that
(\frac{a_{0}}{c_{2,\infty}}r_{m}^{-\frac{1}{2}},q_{m,0},q_{m,1},q_{m,2},q_{m,3})\in\mathcal{T}_{m}
. Note that the constants
\alpha_{i}
are determined in (
5.6
), which ensures
r_{m}^{-\alpha_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}(S_{r_{m}}z)={\varphi_{i}}(z)
.

Altogether, it must be
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}\equiv 0
, which contradicts (
5.26
). This completes the proof.
∎

5.3.
Proof of the main result in flat domains

In this subsection, we will give the proof of the higher order boundary Harnack principle and prove our main result
Theorem 1.1
. A slightly more general version of that result is contained in
Theorem 5.10
.

First, we recall the set
\mathcal{R}^{2}_{-}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{2}_{-}\coloneqq\{v_{n}^{3}\geq 2x_{n}\}.

Now we combine the regularity away from
\gamma_{0}\cup\gamma_{-}
and
subsection 5.2
to obtain the following.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
k\in\{0,1\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)\setminus\{\frac{1}{2}\}
with
k+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
. Let
A\in C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{2})
,
B\in C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{2})
, and
F_{i}\in C^{k+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{2})
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
. Let
f_{i}
be weak solutions to

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f_{i}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{i}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f_{i})&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{i}+F_{i}&&\qquad\mbox{in ${H}_{2}$},\\
f_{i}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap H_{2}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
(A)_{n,n}(0)=1
. Suppose that
f_{2}\geq 0
and that there is
c_{2}\geq 1
such that

(5.44)

\frac{\varphi_{0}}{c_{2}}\leq f_{2}\quad\text{in }H_{2}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{2}_{-}.

Then for any
z_{0}\in H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}
and
r\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{dist}(z_{0},\gamma_{0})}{16}
,

(5.45)

\displaystyle[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{\min\{1+\epsilon,3/2\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})}+[F_{i}]_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{2})}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})},c_{2})
.

Moreover, when
F_{i}\equiv 0
and
k+\epsilon>\frac{1}{2}
, then

(5.46)

\displaystyle[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\max\{\epsilon,k\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})},

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{2})})
.

Note that the condition
k=1
is only relevant in case
F_{0}\equiv 0
. For the first claim (
5.45
), it suffices to consider
k=0
and
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon^{\prime})
for some
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

Let
z_{0}\in H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}
and set
\rho_{0}\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{dist}(z_{0},\gamma_{0})}{8}
. Then, we have

(5.47)

\displaystyle H_{4\rho_{0}}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset\quad\text{and}\quad H_{4\rho_{0}}(z_{0})\subset H_{16\rho_{0}}(\overline{z_{0}}),

where
\overline{z_{0}}\coloneqq(t_{0},x_{0}^{\prime},0,v_{0}^{\prime},0)
. Moreover, by (
5.44
) and the fact that
H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0})\subset H_{2}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{2}
, we have

(5.48)

\displaystyle f_{2}\geq\frac{\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{c}\quad\text{in }H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0})

for some constant
c=c(c_{2})
. Next, we assume

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})}+[F_{i}]_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{2})}\right)\leq 1,

and we omit the dependency of the constant
c
whenever it depends on
n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{1})}
,
\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{1})}
and
c_{2}
. Moreover, by considering
f_{0}(z)\coloneqq f(\overline{z}_{0}\circ z)
, we may assume
\overline{z}_{0}=0
.

By
subsection 5.2
, we have

(5.49)

\displaystyle\|f_{1}-pf_{2}-q{\varphi_{1}}-b_{0}{\varphi_{2}}-b_{1}{\varphi_{3}}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{r})}\leq cr^{\frac{3}{2}+k+\epsilon}

for some
p,q\in\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
and
b_{0},b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
where

(5.50)

(A(0))_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}p(0)=0,

b_{0}=0
when
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
and
q=b_{1}=0
when
\epsilon+k<1
. We now split the proof into several steps.

Step 1.
We identify the equation that is satisfied by
\widetilde{f}\coloneqq f_{1}-pf_{2}-q{\varphi_{1}}-b_{0}{\varphi_{2}}-b_{1}{\varphi_{3}}
.

To this end, we observe from
subsection 2.2
that

\displaystyle f_{2}=c_{0}{\varphi_{0}}+(\mathbf{C}_{0}\cdot v){\varphi_{0}}+(\mathbf{C}_{1}\cdot v){\varphi_{1}}+g

for some
c_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbf{C}_{0},\mathbf{C}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\mathbf{C}_{0}=\mathbf{C}_{1}=0
when
k=0
, and such that

\displaystyle|c_{0}|+|\mathbf{C}_{0}|+|\mathbf{C}_{1}|\leq c,\quad|g(z)|\leq c|z|^{\frac{1}{2}+k+\epsilon}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}=f_{1}-p(0)f_{2}-(\nabla_{v}p\cdot v)c_{0}{\varphi_{0}}-(p(z)-p(0))g(z)-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{3}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}

for some polynomials
q_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{3}
with
q_{0}(0)=q_{1}(0)=Dq_{0}(0)=Dq_{1}(0)=0
and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{3}\|q_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1})}\leq c.

The proof of this fact follows in the same way as the proof of (
5.19
).

Next, we observe that for
\widetilde{p}(z)\coloneqq p(z)-p(0)
,
\widetilde{p}_{0}(z)\coloneqq c_{0}+\mathbf{C}_{0}\cdot v
and
\widetilde{p}_{1}(z)\coloneqq\mathbf{C}_{0}\cdot v
,

(5.51)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\widetilde{p}g)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\widetilde{p}g)-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}g))-B\cdot\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}g)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\widetilde{p}g+\widetilde{p}(\partial_{t}g+v\cdot\nabla_{x}g-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}g)-B\cdot\nabla_{v}g)-2A\nabla_{v}g\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g-(B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\widetilde{p}g+\widetilde{p}\left(F_{2}-\sum_{i=0}^{1}\mathrm{div}_{v}((A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}}))-B\cdot\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})+m_{0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}+\widetilde{p}_{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle-2A\nabla_{v}g\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g-(B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g

for some
\widetilde{p}_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
m_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\widetilde{p}_{2}(0)=0
, where we have used (
2.6
).
We further estimate the right hand side as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{p}g+\widetilde{p}F_{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\left[(\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})^{\mathrm{t}}(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{p}(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}}))\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\widetilde{p}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{1}B\cdot\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})-m_{0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}-\widetilde{p}_{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}\right)-A\nabla_{v}g\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}g)-(B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\widetilde{F}_{g}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{G}_{g}),

where we have used
\mathrm{div}_{v}(Fg)=F\cdot\nabla_{v}g+\mathrm{div}_{v}(F)g
and we write

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{g}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\partial_{t}\widetilde{p}g+\widetilde{p}F_{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}(\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})^{\mathrm{t}}(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})-\sum_{i=0}^{1}\widetilde{p}B\cdot\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})+\widetilde{p}(m_{0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}+\widetilde{p}_{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}})

\displaystyle\quad-A\nabla_{v}g\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}-(B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p})g,

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{g}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{1}\widetilde{p}(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})+A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{p}g.

Now using these definitions, as well as (
2.4
) and (
5.50
), we get

(5.52)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{f}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{f}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{F}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{G})\quad\text{in }H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}),

where we write

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}\coloneqq F_{1}-p(0)F_{2}-q_{2}-\widetilde{F}_{g}-h,\quad\widetilde{G}\coloneqq-\widetilde{G}_{g}-(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}((\nabla_{v}p\cdot v)c_{0}{\varphi_{0}}+q_{2}{\varphi_{2}})

with
h
given by

\displaystyle h\coloneqq\partial_{t}\left(\sum_{i\neq 2}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(\sum_{i\neq 2}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)-\mathrm{div}_{v}\left(A\nabla_{v}\left(\sum_{i\neq 2}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right)\right)-B\cdot\nabla_{v}\left(\sum_{i\neq 2}q_{i}{\varphi_{i}}\right).

Note that this yields

(5.53)

[h]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{4\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq[h]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{16\rho_{0}})}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}.

Indeed, the functions
q_{0},q_{1},q_{3}
are nonzero and
A\in C^{1,\epsilon}
, when
k=1
. Therefore, using the fact that
q_{0}{\varphi_{0}},q_{1}{\varphi_{1}}
, and
{\varphi_{2}}
have degree
\frac{5}{2}
, and (
5.47
) with
\overline{z}_{0}=0
, we derive (
5.53
).

Step 2.
Now, we estimate the
L^{\infty}
norms and Hölder semi-norms of
\widetilde{F}_{g}
and
\widetilde{G}_{g}
. First, we will show

(5.54)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}_{g}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\quad\text{if }k=0,

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{2+\epsilon}[\widetilde{F}_{g}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho^{2+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}\quad\text{if }k=1.

To do so, we need the regularity of
g
and
\nabla_{v}g
, so we investigate the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g+v\cdot\nabla_{x}g-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}g)

\displaystyle=B\cdot\nabla_{v}g+F_{2}-\sum_{i=0}^{1}\left[\mathrm{div}_{v}((A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}}))-B\cdot\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+m_{0}\partial_{v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}}+\widetilde{p}_{2}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}{\varphi_{0}},

which follows from the third line of (
5.51
). By
subsection 5.1
, we have

(5.55)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}g\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{\epsilon}[\nabla_{v}g]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}}\left(\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}\sum_{i=0}^{1}[(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{p}_{i}{\varphi_{i}})]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)+\rho_{0}(\|F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\leq c\rho_{0}^{\min\{-\frac{1}{2}+k+\epsilon,\frac{1}{2}\}},

where we have used the fact that
H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0})\subset H_{16\rho_{0}}
by (
5.47
) with
\overline{z}_{0}=0
, (
2.8
) and

(5.56)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{16\rho_{0}})}\leq c\rho_{0}^{k+\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon},

\displaystyle\|A-A(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq\|A-A(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{16\rho_{0}})}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\min\{k+\epsilon,1\}}.

Similarly, we have

(5.57)

\displaystyle[g]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now using (
5.55
), (
5.56
), and (
5.57
) together with the fact that

\|\widetilde{p}\|_{C^{\delta}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}=\|{p}(z)-p(0)\|_{C^{\delta}(H_{2\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq\|{p}(z)-p(0)\|_{C^{\delta}(H_{16\rho_{0}})}\leq c\rho_{0}^{1-\delta}\quad\text{for any }\delta\in[0,1],

we derive (
5.54
). In particular, for
C^{1,\epsilon}
estimates of the function
\widetilde{G}
, we have used
subsection 2.1
and the product rule for the Hölder space.

Step 3.
Now we are ready to apply
subsection 5.1
to the solution of (
5.52
). To this end, we divide the proof into four cases according to the range of
\epsilon
and the value of
k
.

•

Let
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
k=0
. Then we have
q=b_{0}=b_{1}=0
. Thus, by
subsection 5.1
together with (
5.49
) and (
5.54
), we have

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{1+\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}}\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}(z_{0}))}}+\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, by (
5.47
) with
\overline{z}_{0}=0
and (
5.49
), we have
\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq\rho_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}
. Thus, by
subsection 2.1
we get for any
l\in\{0,1\}
and
\delta\in(0,1)
with
l+\delta\leq 1+\epsilon
,

(5.58)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{l+\delta}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-(l+\delta)}.

•

Let
\epsilon\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and
k=0
. Similarly, we use
subsection 5.1
along with
\delta=\frac{3}{2}
and (
5.49
), (
5.54
), to see that

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}(z_{0}))}}+\rho_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{2}}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{2}}}\left(\rho_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}+\rho_{0}^{2}+\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right),

where we have used the fact that

(5.59)

\displaystyle[(A-A(0))\nabla_{v}{\varphi_{2}}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0},\quad[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}.

In particular, for the first inequality given in (
5.59
), we have used (
2.8
).
Therefore, we have
[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}
. As in the previous case, we also derive (
5.58
) with
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2}
and
l+\delta\leq\frac{3}{2}
.

•

Let
\epsilon\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and assume
F_{i}\equiv 0
. In that case we have
q_{2}=0
. Hence, as in the proof of (
5.58
), we also deduce

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{1+\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}}\left(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2}\|\widetilde{F}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}(z_{0}))}}+\rho_{0}^{1+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\right)\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}

and, whenever
l+\delta<2
,

(5.60)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{l+\delta}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon-(l+\delta)}.

•

Let
k=1
and
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. Since
q_{2}=0
, we deduce from
subsection 5.1
, (
5.49
), and (
5.54
) that

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{2+\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c}{\rho_{0}^{2+\epsilon}}(\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2+\epsilon}[\widetilde{F}]_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}+\rho_{0}^{2+\epsilon}[\widetilde{G}]_{C^{1+\epsilon}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))})\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon}.

Moreover, as
\|\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{\rho_{0}/2}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon}
by (
5.49
), using
subsection 2.1
, we deduce that for any
l+\delta\leq 2+\epsilon
,

(5.61)

\displaystyle[\widetilde{f}]_{C^{l+\delta}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}-(l+\delta)}.

Step 4.
We are ready to prove the main estimates.
First, note that

(5.62)

\displaystyle[f_{2}]_{C^{l,\delta}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{-(l+\delta)}

for any
l\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\delta\in[0,1]
with
l+\delta\leq 1+\epsilon
when
k=0
, and
l+\delta<2+\epsilon
when
k=1
by
subsection 5.1
.

Thus, using this together with
subsection 2.1
, the product rule and (
5.44
), we have

(5.63)

\displaystyle[1/f_{2}]_{C^{l,\delta}(H_{\rho_{0}}(z_{0}))}\leq c\rho_{0}^{-(l+\delta)-\frac{1}{2}},

by
subsection 2.2
and due to the lower bound (
5.48
). Now we write
r\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{dist}(z_{0},\gamma_{0})}{16}=\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}
.

For
k=0
, we observe

\displaystyle[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}=[(\widetilde{f}+q{\varphi_{1}}+b_{0}{\varphi_{2}}+b_{1}{\varphi_{3}})/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}.

Using
subsection 2.1
,
subsection 2.1
, the product rule, (
5.58
), (
5.62
), and (
5.63
), we get

(5.64)

\displaystyle{}_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq c[\nabla_{v}(\widetilde{f}/f_{2})]_{C^{\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}+c[\widetilde{f}/f_{2}]_{C_{\mathcal{T}}^{{1+\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle+c[\widetilde{f}/f_{2}]_{C_{x}^{{1+\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c,

where we have used
[f]_{{C_{\mathcal{T}}^{{1+\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}}+[f]_{C_{x}^{{1+\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c[f]_{C^{1+\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}
by the definition of the kinetic Hölder space given in (
2.2
). In addition, by (
4.1
), (
2.8
), and (
2.8
), we have

\displaystyle[(q{\varphi_{1}}+b_{0}{\varphi_{2}}+b_{1}{\varphi_{3}})/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{3}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c.

Altogether, this proves (
5.45
).

For (
5.46
), we assume
F_{i}\equiv 0
,
\epsilon\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, and
k=0
. Then we have
q\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
with
q(0)=Dq(0)=0
. Thus we have

\displaystyle[(q{\varphi_{1}}+b_{1}{\varphi_{3}})/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c.

Moreover, as in (
5.64
) together with (
5.60
), we have
[\widetilde{f}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\epsilon}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c
. Thus, we get (
5.46
) when
\epsilon\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.

Now we assume
F_{i}\equiv 0
,
k=0
, and
\epsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. We will prove
[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{2,\epsilon}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq cr^{-\epsilon}
, which gives
[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,1}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c
using
subsection 2.1
together with
\|f_{1}/f_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq cr^{2}
. Note that the
L^{\infty}
-estimate follows from (
5.49
), (
5.44
), and the fact that
q{\varphi_{1}}
and
{\varphi_{3}}
have homogeneity
\frac{5}{2}
, by (
2.8
) and the fact that
q(0)=Dq(0)=0
.

As in (
5.64
) together with (
5.61
) instead of (
5.58
), we derive
[\widetilde{f}/f_{2}]_{C^{2,\epsilon}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq cr^{-\epsilon}
. Moreover using the homogeneity of
q{\varphi_{1}}
and
{\varphi_{3}}
, we deduce
[\widetilde{f}/f_{2}]_{C^{2,\epsilon}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq cr^{-\epsilon}
. Thus, as in the above argument, we derive the desired estimate (
5.46
) when
k=1
, which completes the proof.
∎

Now we combine
Theorem 4.3
,
subsection 5.3
and the covering argument given in
[
53
, Lemma 2.9]
to prove the main result of this article in the half-space.

Proof of
Theorem 5.1
.

First, we claim that the Hopf lemma
Theorem 4.3
remains true in larger domains
H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{M}_{-}
instead of
H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}
, where
\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}\coloneqq\{Mx_{n}\leq v_{n}^{3}\}
for any
M\geq 1
. Indeed, we get (
4.29
) with
\mathcal{R}_{-}
replaced by
\mathcal{R}^{M}_{-}
, as
\frac{1}{c}|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq{\varphi_{0}}(z)\leq c|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}
in the complement of
\mathcal{R}^{M}_{-}
for some constant
c=c(M)
, and then we just follow the lines of the remaining proof given in
Theorem 4.3
.
Therefore, we have in particular,

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{c}\leq\inf_{H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{2}_{-}}\frac{f}{{\varphi_{0}}},

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{2})})
. By applying a re-scaled version of
subsection 5.3
, for any
z_{0}\in H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{2}_{-}
and
r\coloneqq\frac{\mathrm{dist}(z_{0},\gamma_{0})}{16}
, we deduce

\displaystyle[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{1}{2}\}}(H_{r}(z_{0}))}\leq c\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})}+[F_{i}]_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{2})}\right)\leq c

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{2},\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})})
, as
\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2})}+\|F_{i}\|_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(H_{2})}\leq 1
. Next, by the covering argument given in
[
53
, Lemma 2.9]
, we derive

\displaystyle[f_{1}/f_{2}]_{C^{1,\min\{\epsilon,\frac{1}{2}\}}(H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-})}\leq c.

When
F_{i}\equiv 0
, the arguments are exactly the same so that we also obtain (
5.2
).
∎

5.4.
Boundary Harnack in non-flat domains

We conclude this subsection by providing the boundary Harnack principle in general domains. Since the same flattening argument is already explained in the proof of
Theorem 1.5
, we do not give all details here.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain of class
C^{\frac{k+4+\epsilon}{2}}
for some
k\in\{0,1\}
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)\setminus\{\frac{1}{2}\}
with
k+\epsilon<\frac{3}{2}
and
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
. Let
A\in C^{k+\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{k+\epsilon-1}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))
,
F_{i}\in C^{k+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
, where
\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})\coloneqq H_{2}(z_{0})\cap\{x\in\Omega\}
. Let
f_{i}
be weak solutions to

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f_{i}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{i}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f_{i})&=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{i}+F_{i}&&\qquad\mbox{in $\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})$},\\
f_{i}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\gamma_{-}\cap\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})$}.\end{aligned}\right.

with
f_{2},F_{2}\geq 0
. Suppose
\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|F_{i}\|_{C^{\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
. Then, there exist a small constant
\rho_{1}=\rho_{1}(\Lambda,\Omega)
and a large constant
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,\Lambda,\Omega)
such that if
f_{2}(z_{1})=c_{0}>0
for some
c_{0}>0
with
z_{1}\coloneqq z_{0}\circ(-1,-\rho_{1}^{3}n_{x_{0}},0)
and

\displaystyle\|F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\mathcal{M}},

then

\displaystyle\left[\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right]_{C^{\min\{1+\epsilon,3/2\}}(\mathbf{H}_{\frac{\rho_{1}}{8}}(z_{0})\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-})}\leq c,

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{0},\|A\|_{C^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{\epsilon-1}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},|z_{0}|,\Omega)
and
\mathcal{R}_{-}\coloneqq\{d_{\Omega}(x)\leq(n_{x}\cdot v)^{3}\}
.

Moreover, when
F_{i}\equiv 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right]_{C^{1,\max\{\epsilon,k\}}(\mathbf{H}_{\frac{\rho_{1}}{8}}(z_{0})\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-})}\leq c

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{0},\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},|z_{0}|,\Omega)
.

Proof.

We may assume
z_{0}=0
as
z_{0}\in\gamma_{0}
. As in the proof of
Theorem 1.5
,
\widetilde{f}_{i}\coloneqq f_{i}\circ\Psi^{-1}
is a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\widetilde{f}_{i}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{f}_{i}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(\widetilde{A}\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{i})&=\widetilde{B}\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{f}_{i}+\widetilde{F}_{i}&&\qquad\mbox{in $I_{2}\times\mathrm{H}_{2R_{1}}$},\\
\widetilde{f}_{i}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\widetilde{A}
,
\widetilde{B}
and
\widetilde{F}
are defined as in
[
38
, (8.3)]
. Similarly, as in the proof of
Theorem 1.5
, we may assume
(\widetilde{A}(0))_{n,n}=1
and we choose
\rho_{1}=\rho_{1}(\Omega)
such that
\Psi(-1,x_{0}-\rho_{1}^{3}n_{x_{0}},0)=(-1,0,(\rho R_{1})^{3},0)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\rho
is the universal constant determined in
Theorem 5.1
to have
f_{2}(-1,0,(\rho R_{1})^{3},0)=c_{0}
. Next, selecting a small constant
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,\Lambda,\Omega)
, we have
\widetilde{f}_{2}(-(R_{1})^{3},0,(\rho R_{1})^{3},0)=\frac{c_{0}}{c}
for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,\Omega)
. From
[
53
, Lemma 2.17]
,
\widetilde{A}\in C^{k+\epsilon},\widetilde{B}\in C^{k+\epsilon-1}
, and
\widetilde{F}\in C^{k+\epsilon-\frac{1}{2}}
as
\partial\Omega\in C^{\frac{k+4+\epsilon}{2}}
. Thus, by applying a rescaled version of
Theorem 5.1
, we deduce Hölder estimates of
\widetilde{f}_{1}/\widetilde{f}_{2}
. Finally, scaling back and using the fact that
\Psi^{-1}(\{x_{n}\leq(v_{n})^{3}\})\subset\mathcal{R}_{-}
, we obtain the desired result. Lastly, since we have assumed
(\widetilde{A}(0))_{n,n}=1
by considering
\widetilde{f}_{a}(z)(=\widetilde{f}(t,ax,av))
as in the proof of
Theorem 1.5
, the constant
\rho_{1}
eventually depends on
\Omega,\Lambda
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem 1.1
.

Theorem 1.1
is a special case of
Theorem 5.10
.
∎

5.5.
Sharpness of our main result

We discuss the sharpness of our main result. First, we give a remark on the possibility to enlarge the domain in which the boundary Harnack estimate is valid.

Remark 5.11
.

We note that our result also holds for a larger domain, whenever the domain is away from
\gamma_{-}
. More precisely, we can obtain the Hölder estimates of the quotient in
H_{\rho/8}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}
for any
M>1
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}=\{Mx_{n}\leq v_{n}^{3}\}.

To do this, as we pointed out in the proof of
Theorem 5.1
, we can derive

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}}{c}\leq\inf_{H_{\frac{\rho}{8}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}}\frac{f}{{\varphi_{0}}}

for any
M\geq 1
, where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{2})},M)
. Thus, by the same argument as in the proof of
subsection 5.3
, we have (
5.45
) and (
5.46
) whenever
z_{0}\in H_{\rho/8}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{M}_{-}
. Therefore, we can derive the Hölder estimates given in
Theorem 5.1
with
\mathcal{R}_{-}
replaced by
\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}
. In that case, the constant
c
depends on
n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{0},\|A\|_{C^{k+\epsilon}(H_{2})},\|B\|_{C^{k+\epsilon-1}(H_{2})}
, and on
M
.

Now, we discuss the optimality of the regularity exponents in
Theorem 5.10
.

Example 5.12
.

First note that
\phi_{0}=\phi_{0}(x_{n},v_{n})
solves (
5.1
) with
A=I
,
B=0
, and
F_{2}=0
. Moreover, we have
\phi_{0}(z_{0})=c_{0}>0
for some universal constant
c_{0}
.

Now we construct suitable solutions to verify the sharpness of the result given in
Theorem 5.10
.

•

Let
F_{1}\equiv 1
. We use the function
\psi_{0}(x_{n},v_{n})
that solves the equation (
5.1
) with
A=I
,
B=0
, and
F_{1}=1
(see (
2.4
)). However, by
[
38
, Lemma C.1, (C.5)]
and
[
38
, Lemma C.6]
, we deduce
\phi_{0}(0,x_{n})=c_{0}x_{n}^{\frac{1}{6}}
and
\psi_{0}(0,x_{n})=c_{1}x_{n}^{\frac{2}{3}}
, which implies

\psi_{0}/\phi_{0}\notin C^{\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}(H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-})
for any
\epsilon>0
.

•

Let
F_{1}\equiv 0
. We observe from (
2.5
) that
f_{1}\coloneqq\frac{v_{1}^{2}}{2m_{0}}\phi_{0}(x_{n},v_{n})-\partial_{v_{n}}\phi_{1}(x_{n},v_{n})
solves (
5.1
) with
A=I
,
B=0
, and
F_{1}=0
. Moreover, from
[
38
, Lemma C.1 and (C.1)]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}}{\phi_{0}}=\frac{v_{1}^{2}}{2m_{0}}+c_{2}v_{n}^{2}\frac{U(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{5}{3},-\frac{v_{n}^{3}}{9x_{n}})}{U(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{2}{3},-\frac{v_{n}^{3}}{9x_{n}})}

for some constant
c_{2}\neq 0
when
v_{n}\leq 0
. For any
c>0
, we can find sequences
(a_{i})_{i}
,
(b_{i})_{i}
such that
-\frac{(a_{i})^{3}}{b_{i}}\to c
with
a_{i},b_{i}\to 0
as
i\to\infty
. In particular, by choosing
|a_{i}|^{3}>b_{i}
and
z_{i}=(t_{i},x_{i}^{\prime},x_{i,n},v_{i}^{\prime},v_{i,n})\coloneqq(0,0,b_{i},0,a_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{f_{1}(z_{i})}{\phi_{0}(z_{i})}\frac{1}{|v_{n}|^{2}}=c_{2}\frac{U(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{5}{3},c)}{U(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{2}{3},c)},

which implies that
\lim\limits_{z\to 0}\partial_{v_{n},v_{n}}(f_{1}/\phi_{0})(z)
is not unique. Thus,
f_{1}/\phi_{0}\notin C^{2}(H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-})
.

5.6.
Behavior away from the grazing set

Finally, we discuss the boundary behavior of
f_{1}/f_{2}
away from
\gamma_{0}
.
The following proposition shows that
f_{1}/f_{2}
is smooth near
\gamma_{+}
but away from
\gamma_{0}
.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain of class
C^{\frac{k+4+\epsilon}{2}}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
z_{0}\in\gamma_{+}
and
\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})\cap\gamma_{0}=\emptyset
. Let
A\in C^{k-1,\epsilon}(H_{2}(z_{0}))
,
B\in C^{k-2,\epsilon}(H_{2}(z_{0}))
and
F\in C^{k-2,\epsilon}(H_{2}(z_{0}))
. Let
f_{i}
be a weak solution to

\partial_{t}f_{i}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{i}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f_{i})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{i}+F_{i}\quad\mbox{in $\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})$}

with
f_{2},F_{2}\geq 0
. Suppose
\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|F_{i}\|_{C^{k-2,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
. Then, there exist a small constant
\rho_{1}=\rho_{1}(\Omega)
and a large constant
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,\Lambda,\Omega)
such that if
f_{2}(z_{1})=c_{0}>0
for some
c_{0}>0
with
z_{1}\coloneqq z_{0}\circ(-1,0,0)
and

(5.65)

\displaystyle\|F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\mathcal{M}},

then

\displaystyle\left[\frac{f_{1}}{f_{2}}\right]_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{0}))}\leq c,

where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,c_{0},\|A\|_{C^{k-1,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{k-2,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},|z_{0}|,\Omega)
.

Proof.

Since the flattening argument given in the proof of
Theorem 1.5
preserves the regularity of the coefficients
A,B
and the right-hand side
F
, by
subsection 2.2
together with the assumption
\|f_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|F_{i}\|_{C^{k-2,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq 1
and the interpolation in
subsection 2.1
, we have
\|f_{i}\|_{C^{k,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{1}(z_{0}))}\leq c
, where
c=c(n,\Lambda,\epsilon,\|A\|_{C^{k-1,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},\|B\|_{C^{k-2,\epsilon}(\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0}))},|z_{0}|,\Omega)
.

We are now going to prove
f_{2}(z)\geq\frac{c_{0}}{c}
in
\mathbf{H}_{\rho_{1}}(z_{0})
for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda,c_{0},\Omega)
provided that
f_{2}(z_{1})=c_{0}
with
z_{1}=z_{0}\circ(-1,0,0)
and (
5.65
) for some suitable constants
\rho_{1}\leq 1
and
\mathcal{M}\geq 1
.

As the flattening procedure is well explained in
Theorem 1.5
, we may assume
\mathbf{H}_{2}(z_{0})=H_{2}(z_{0})
,
f_{2}(z_{1})=c_{0}
and
\|F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\mathcal{M}}
. Thus, it suffices to show that
f_{2}(z)\geq\frac{c_{0}}{c}
in
H_{\rho}(z_{0})
for some universal constant
\rho
. By following the extension argument as in (
3.26
), we also assume that
f_{2}
is actually a weak solution to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{2}-\mathrm{div}_{v}(A\nabla_{v}f_{2})=B\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{2}+F_{2},\quad\text{in }Q_{2}(z_{0}),

with
{f}_{2}(z_{0}\circ(-1,0,0))=c_{0}
and
\|F_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{2}(z_{0}))}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{\mathcal{M}}
. Now, taking
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,\Lambda)
sufficiently small and following (
3.27
) with
z_{1}=z_{0}\circ(-1,0,0)
, we deduce
{f}_{2}(z_{0}\circ(-\epsilon_{0}^{2},0,0))\geq\frac{c_{0}}{c}
. Next, by applying
section 3
with
z_{1}=z_{0}\circ(-\epsilon_{0}^{2},0,0)
and
\rho=1
, we have

\inf\limits_{Q_{r_{0}}(z_{0})}{f}_{2}(z)\geq\frac{c_{0}}{c}

for some constant
c=c(n,\Lambda)
and universal constant
r_{0}
, whenever
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}(n,\Lambda)
is sufficiently large. By selecting
\rho=r_{0}
, we have the desired lower bound of
f_{2}
in
H_{\rho}(z_{0})
, which completes the proof.
∎

The following example shows that our main result
Theorem 5.1
together with
subsection 5.5
is sharp, in the sense that there is a solution
f
such that the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
f/\phi_{0}
in
H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}
explodes when
M\to\infty
. Moreover, we observe that the boundary Harnack principle in
\mathcal{R}_{-}
fails for any reference point in
\mathcal{R}_{-}
.

Example 5.14
.

Let
f\coloneqq{\phi}_{-1}\xi
with
\xi=\xi(v)\in C^{\infty}
such that
\xi(v)\equiv 0
for
v\leq\frac{1}{32}
and
\xi(v)\equiv 1
for
v>\frac{1}{16}
, where
\phi_{-1}
is given in
[
38
, Lemma C.1]
. Moreover, by
[
38
, Lemma C.1]
, we have that
\phi_{-1}
is a weak solution to
(v\partial_{x}-\partial_{vv})\phi_{-1}=0
in
\{x>0\}\times\{v>\frac{1}{16}\}
. Thus,
f
is a weak solution to

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f+v\partial_{x}f-\partial_{vv}f&=F&&\qquad\mbox{in $H_{1}$},\\
f&=0&&\qquad\mbox{on $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$},\end{aligned}\right.

where
F=-\partial_{vv}\xi\phi_{-1}-2\partial_{v}\xi\partial_{v}\phi_{-1}
. In addition, from the computations given in
[
38
, Lemma C.1]
, we deduce
F\in C^{\infty}(H_{1})
, as the support of
F
is away from the origin. Moreover, we have
\phi_{0}(-1,\rho^{3},0)=\phi_{0}(\rho^{3},0)=c\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}>0
, where
\rho
is the universal constant determined in
Theorem 5.1
. However, we observe from
[
38
, Lemma C.5]
that

\displaystyle\|f/\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{-})}\geq\|(x^{-1}v^{\frac{1}{2}})/(xv^{-\frac{5}{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{-})}=\infty.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|f/\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}^{M}_{-})}\eqsim M^{2}.

In addition, for any
C>0
, we can find
(x_{1},v_{1}),(x_{2},v_{2})\in\mathcal{R}_{-}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{0}(x_{1},v_{1})}{\phi_{-1}(x_{1},v_{1})}\frac{\phi_{-1}(x_{2},v_{2})}{\phi_{0}(x_{2},v_{2})}=\frac{x_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{-3}}{x_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{-3}}\geq C.

For instance, this can be achieved by choosing
(x_{1},v_{1})=(1/10,1/2)
and
(x_{2},v_{2})=(1/(10\sqrt{C}),1/2)
.
This indicates that we cannot chose any reference point so that the boundary Harnack principle holds in
H_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{-}^{M}
.

6.
Appendix

This section is motivated by
[
38
, Theorem 1.7]
(see also
[
38
, Theorem 8.6]
), where we constructed a function
\Phi
, which behaves like the square-root of the kinetic distance function to
\gamma_{0}
(see (
6.1
) for a precise definition of
\Phi
), such that
f/\Phi\in C^{0,1}
in
H_{1}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}
. Here, we construct an explicit example indicating that the Lipschitz regularity obtained in
[
38
]
was optimal. Thereby, it becomes apparent that the higher regularity of order
C^{3/2}
and
C^{1,1}
obtained in this article is a specific feature of the quotient of two solutions.

Example 6.1
.

Let us fix a domain
H_{\frac{1}{4}}=(0,1/4^{3})\times(-1/4,1/4)
. Define

\displaystyle a(v)\coloneqq 1+v,\,\varphi(x,v)\coloneqq v\phi_{0}-v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+5\phi_{0}

to see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} v\partial_{x}\varphi-\partial_{v}(a(v)\partial_{v}\varphi)&=b\partial_{v}\varphi+F&&\qquad\mbox{in $H_{\frac{1}{4}}$},\\
{\varphi}_{1}&=0&&\qquad\mbox{in $\{x_{n}=0\}\times\{v_{n}>0\}$}\end{aligned}\right.

for some
F\in L^{\infty}(H_{1/4})
, where
b\coloneqq(-\partial_{v}(a(v))
.
To this end, we note that

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}\phi_{0}-\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}=0,\,v\partial_{x}(v\phi_{0})-\partial^{2}_{v}(v\phi_{0})=-2\partial_{v}\phi_{0},

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}(v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0})-\partial^{2}_{v}(v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0})=-2\partial_{v}\phi_{0}-5v\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0},\,\partial^{2}_{v}(v\phi_{0})=2\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+v\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0},

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{v}(v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0})=2\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+4v\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}+v^{2}\partial_{v}^{3}\phi_{0}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle v\partial_{x}\varphi-\partial_{v}(a(v)\partial_{v}\varphi)-b\partial_{v}=v\partial_{x}\varphi-a(v)\partial^{2}_{v}\varphi

\displaystyle=v\partial_{x}\varphi-\partial^{2}_{v}\varphi+(1-a(v))\partial_{v}^{2}\varphi

\displaystyle=5v\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0}+(-v)(-3v\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0}-v^{2}\partial_{v}^{3}\phi_{0}+5\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0})

\displaystyle=3v^{2}\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0}+v^{3}\partial_{v}^{3}\phi_{0}\eqqcolon F(x,v)

and the function
F
is bounded. Now we claim that for

(6.1)

\displaystyle\Phi(x,v)\coloneqq\phi_{0}(x/{\mathbf{a}(v)},v/\mathbf{a}(v))

with
\mathbf{a}(v)\coloneqq\sqrt{a(v)}
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi}{\Phi}\notin C^{1}(\overline{H_{1/4}}\setminus\mathcal{R}_{-}),

where
\mathcal{R}_{-}=\{x\leq v^{3}\}
.
To this end, we fix two sequences
(x_{i})
and
(v_{i})
such that
x_{i}\to 0
and
v_{i}\to 0
with
(v_{i})^{3}\leq x_{i}
,
-\frac{(v_{i})^{3}}{9x_{i}}\to z
for some constant
z
.

First, we prove

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\Phi(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\phi_{0}(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}},

where we write
\rho_{i}\coloneqq\max\{|x_{i}|^{\frac{1}{3}},|v_{i}|\}
. To this end, note that by the fundamental theorem of calculus

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x_{i}/\mathbf{a}(v_{i}),v_{i}/\mathbf{a}(v_{i}))-\phi_{0}(x_{i},v_{i})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{x}\phi_{0}(x_{i}(\xi/\mathbf{a}(v_{i})+(1-\xi)),v_{i}/\mathbf{a}(v_{i}))\times x_{i}(1/\mathbf{a}(v_{i})-1)\,d\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}(x_{i},v_{i}(\xi/\mathbf{a}(v_{i})+1-\xi))\times v_{i}(1/\mathbf{a}(v_{i})-1)\,d\xi.

Now using this together the fact that
|x\partial_{x}\phi_{0}(x,v)|,|v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}(x,v)|\leq c(|x|^{\frac{1}{6}}+|v|^{\frac{1}{2}})
and
|1/\mathbf{a}(v)-1|\leq c|v|
for some constant
c
, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{|\Phi(x_{i}^{3},v_{i})-\phi(x_{i}^{3},v_{i})|}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq c|v_{i}|.

With this estimate, we can derive (
6.3
). Now using the explicit formula of
\phi_{0}
given in
[
38
, Appendix C]
, we have when
-\frac{(v_{i})^{3}}{9x_{i}}\to z\geq 0
with
v_{i}\leq 0
,

(6.4)

\displaystyle 0<g_{0}(z)\coloneqq\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\Phi(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\phi_{0}(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\begin{cases}c_{0}U\left(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{2}{3},z\right)&\quad\text{if }z\leq\frac{1}{9},\\
c_{0}\left(\frac{1}{9z}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}U\left(-\frac{1}{6},\frac{2}{3},z\right)&\quad\text{if }z\geq\frac{1}{9}.\end{cases}

Next, we prove

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=1,

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=\lim_{i\to\infty}\left[2\left(\frac{v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})+\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})-\left(\frac{v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)^{2}(x_{i},v_{i})\right],

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=\lim_{i\to\infty}\left[\frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})+\frac{2}{3}\left(\frac{v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)^{2}(x_{i},v_{i})\right].

To do this, we observe

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)=(\phi_{0}+v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})\Phi^{-1}-\phi_{0}(v\partial_{v}\Phi)\Phi^{-2}.

As in (
6.3
), we get

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{(v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})(x_{i},v_{i})}{|v_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{(v\partial_{v}\Phi_{0})(x_{i},v_{i})}{|v_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Using this and (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=1.

Next, we observe

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)=(2v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0})\Phi^{-1}-(v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})(v\partial_{v}\Phi)\Phi^{-2},

which implies

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=\lim_{i\to\infty}\left[2\left(\frac{v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})+\left(\frac{v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})-\left(\frac{v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}}{\phi_{0}}\right)^{2}(x_{i},v_{i})\right].

Now we are going to estimate

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\phi_{0}}{\Phi}\right)=(\partial_{v}\phi_{0})\Phi^{-1}-\phi_{0}(\partial_{v}\Phi)\Phi^{-2}=\frac{(\Phi-\phi_{0})(\partial_{v}\phi_{0})}{\Phi^{2}}-\frac{\phi_{0}\partial_{v}(\Phi-\phi_{0})}{\Phi^{2}}\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2}.

To show the limit of
J_{1}+J_{2}
, we observe

(6.6)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(x,v)-\Phi(x,v)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}(x,v)-\phi_{0}(x/\mathbf{a},v)+\phi_{0}(x/\mathbf{a},v)-\phi_{0}(x/\mathbf{a},v/\mathbf{a})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{x}\phi_{0}(\xi x+(1-\xi)x/\mathbf{a},v)\times x(1-1/\mathbf{a})\,d\xi

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}(x/\mathbf{a},\xi v+(1-\xi)v/\mathbf{a})\times v(1-1/\mathbf{a})\,d\xi

and

\lim_{v\to 0}\frac{1-1/\mathbf{a}(v)}{v}=1.

As in (
6.3
), we deduce

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left[(\phi_{0}-\Phi)(\partial_{v}\phi_{0})\right](x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left[(x\partial_{x}\phi_{0}+v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})(v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})\right](x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}.

Similarly, as in (
6.6
) with
\phi_{0}
and
\Phi
replaced by
\partial_{v}\phi_{0}
and
\partial_{v}\Phi
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left[\phi_{0}\partial_{v}(\phi_{0}-\Phi)\right](x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left[(vx\partial_{x}\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0})\phi_{0}\right](x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}.

Now, we combine the previous two limits and the fact that

\displaystyle 3x\partial_{x}\phi_{0}+v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\phi_{0}

by the homogeneity of
\phi_{0}
, in order to obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left(\Phi\partial_{v}\phi_{0}-\phi_{0}\partial_{v}\Phi\right)(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{\left[(\frac{1}{6}\phi_{0}+\frac{2}{3}v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})(v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})+\left(-\frac{1}{6}v\partial_{v}\phi_{0}+\frac{2}{3}v^{2}\partial_{v}^{2}\phi_{0}\right)\phi_{0}\right](x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|}.

So far, we have verified (
6.5
).

Furthermore, we note from the computations given in
[
38
, Proof of Lemma C.1]
and (C.1) in
[
38
, Appendix C]
that if
-\frac{v_{i}^{3}}{x_{i}}\to z\geq 0
with
v_{i}\to 0
and
x_{i}\to 0
, then

\displaystyle g_{1}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{(v\partial_{v}\phi_{0})(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\begin{cases}c_{0}{\frac{z}{2}U\left(\frac{5}{6},\frac{5}{3},z\right)}&\quad\text{if }z\leq\frac{1}{9},\\
c_{0}\left(\frac{1}{9z}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\frac{z}{2}U\left(\frac{5}{6},\frac{5}{3},z\right)&\quad\text{if }z\geq\frac{1}{9}\end{cases}

\displaystyle g_{2}(z)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{(v^{2}\partial^{2}_{v}\phi_{0})(x_{i},v_{i})}{|\rho_{i}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\begin{cases}c_{0}\left({zU\left(\frac{5}{6},\frac{5}{3},z\right)}-z^{2}\frac{5}{4}U\left(\frac{11}{6},\frac{8}{3},z\right)\right)&\quad\text{if }z\leq\frac{1}{9},\\
c_{0}\left(\frac{1}{9z}\right)^{\frac{1}{6}}\left({zU\left(\frac{5}{6},\frac{5}{3},z\right)}-z^{2}\frac{5}{4}U\left(\frac{11}{6},\frac{8}{3},z\right)\right)&\quad\text{if }z\geq\frac{1}{9}.\end{cases}

Therefore, applying these limits to (
6.5
) leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\varphi}{\Phi}\right)(x_{i},v_{i})=1-2\frac{g_{1}(z)}{g_{0}(z)}-\frac{1}{3}\frac{g_{2}(z)}{g_{0}(z)}+\frac{5}{3}\left(\frac{g_{1}(z)}{g_{0}(z)}\right)^{2}.

whenever
-\frac{v_{i}}{9x_{i}}\to z
with
v_{i}\leq 0
,
v_{i},x_{i}\to 0
.
Now, note from
[
38
, (C.5)-(C.8) in Appendix C]
that

\displaystyle\frac{g_{1}}{g_{0}}(0)=\frac{g_{2}}{g_{0}}(0)=0\quad\lim_{z\to\infty}\frac{g_{1}}{g_{0}}(z)=\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{z\to\infty}\frac{g_{2}}{g_{0}}(z)=-\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, using this and the continuity of the function
g_{i}(z)
together with
g_{0}(z)>0
, there are sequences
\{(x_{1,i},0)\}_{i}
and
\{(x_{2,i},v_{2,i})\}_{i}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\varphi}{\Phi}\right)(x_{1,i},0)=1>\lim_{i\to\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{\varphi}{\Phi}\right)(x_{2,i},v_{2,i}),

where
x_{1,i},x_{2,i},v_{2,i}\to 0
with
-\frac{(v_{2,i})^{3}}{x_{2,i}}\to c
for large constant
c>1
. This verifies (
6.2
).
